## Question #369

You use Power Query Editor to ingest data into Power BI Desktop. The dataset contains 250,000 rows with Value (e.g., 78000.00), Datetime (e.g., 2024-08-15 00:00:00.000), and Information columns. You need to optimize the data model size.

**Answer Area:**

| Column   | Data type    |
|----------|--------------|
| Value    | Whole number |
| Datetime | Date         |

**Correct Answer: Whole number / Date**

---

## Question #370

You have a Power BI semantic model that contains a table named Table1. Table1 runs in Import storage mode. You need to update the model to change the storage mode of Table1 to DirectQuery.

**What should you do?**

- **A) Delete Table1, and then add the table to the model again in DirectQuery storage mode. ✅ (Correct)**
- B) From Power Query Editor, duplicate the query for Table1.
- C) From Model view, change the storage mode of Table1 to DirectQuery.
- D) Add an aggregation table to the model.

**Correct Answer: A**

---

## Question #371

You have a Power BI semantic model with Date, Sales, Survey Response, Customer, and Geography tables. All tables use the same data source. You need to reduce the number of limited relationships in the model by changing the storage mode of certain tables. The solution must NOT affect the latency of the data.

**Answer Area:**

| Table      | Storage mode |
|------------|--------------|
| Customer   | Dual         |
| Geography  | DirectQuery  |

**Correct Answer: Dual / DirectQuery**

---

## Question #372

You have a semantic model that contains a table named Sales with Order ID, Product ID, Sales Date, and Sales Amount columns. You need to create a DAX measure that will return the total sales amount for the same dates from the previous year.

```
PrevYearTotalSalesAmount = CALCULATE (SUM(Sales[Sales Amount]),
    SAMEPERIODLASTYEAR (Sales[Sales Date]))
```

**Correct Answer: CALCULATE / SAMEPERIODLASTYEAR**

---

## Question #373

You have a Power BI semantic model that contains a table named Employees with EmployeeID, HireDate, and TermDate columns. An employee is considered active if the TermDate column contains no value. You need to create a DAX measure that will count the number of active employees.

```
Actives = CALCULATE (COUNTROWS(Employees),
    ISBLANK ('Employees' [TermDate]))
```

**Correct Answer: CALCULATE / ISBLANK**

---

## Question #374

You have a Power BI semantic model with Dim Student, Semester Enrollment, and Dim Address tables. Dim Address relates to Semester Enrollment via both Billing Address ID and Mailing Address ID.

**Which type of dimension is represented by Dim Address?**

- A) Type 2 slowly changing dimension (SCD)
- B) junk dimension
- **C) role-playing dimension ✅ (Correct)**
- D) degenerate dimension

**Correct Answer: C**

---

## Question #375

You are creating a visual in a Power BI report. The associated semantic model contains sales from the last four years for three products. You need to display the total sales by quarter categorized by product. The visual must display trends in both total sales and individual product sales.

**Which type of visual should you use?**

- A) area chart
- B) clustered column chart
- **C) stacked area chart ✅ (Correct)**
- D) line chart

**Correct Answer: C**

---

## Question #376

You have a Power BI report that contains a clustered bar chart with an X-axis named Sales and a Y-axis named Year (2020–2024). You need to create a visual calculation to show a cumulative total of sales equal to the sum of the current year's sales plus all the sales from the previous year.

**Which DAX expression should you use?**

- **A) MOVINGAVERAGE([Sales], 4) ✅ (Correct)**
- B) RUNNINGSUM([Year])
- C) CALCULATE ( [Sales], 'Date' [Year] <= MAX ( 'Date'[Year] ) )
- D) RUNNINGSUM([Sales])

**Correct Answer: A**

---

## Question #377

You are using Power BI Desktop to profile data in Power Query Editor. You need to review the percentage of errors and the maximum value of each column.

**Answer Area:**

| Requirement                        | Option         |
|------------------------------------|----------------|
| Review the percentage of errors    | Column quality |
| Review the maximum value           | Column profile |

**Correct Answer: Column quality / Column profile**

---

## Question #378

You are creating a Power BI report that will contain multiple visuals. You need to ensure that the report is accessible to users who use a screen reader.

**Which two configurations should you perform?**

- A) consistent fonts for each visual
- **B) alt text for each visual ✅ (Correct)**
- **C) the tab order for each page ✅ (Correct)**
- D) consistent colors for each visual
- E) Play Axis for each page

**Correct Answer: BC**

---

## Question #379

You have a Power BI report that contains a stacked bar chart. You need to add a new field to the legend. The field must combine information from separate columns.

**What should you create?**

- **A) a calculated column ✅ (Correct)**
- B) a dynamic format string
- C) a measure
- D) a calculation group

**Correct Answer: A**

---

## Question #380

You have a Fabric workspace named Workspace1 that contains two Power BI reports named Report1 and Report2. You create a workspace app that contains both reports. You need to ensure:

- Report1 must be visible to all the users in your organization.
- Report2 must be visible to only a security group named Group1.

**What should you do next?**

- A) Assign Group1 the Viewer role for Workspace1.
- **B) Create an audience and assign Group1 to the audience. ✅ (Correct)**
- C) Allow access to the hidden content of the app.
- D) Share Report1 with the built-in All users security group.

**Correct Answer: B**

---

## Question #381

You have a Fabric workspace containing Headcount (Report), Headcount (Semantic model), Company Expenses (Report), Expenses (Semantic model), and Department Expenses (Report).

Requirements:
- Management must have view access to Headcount and Department Expenses reports only; cannot build new reports.
- Accounting must only view Company Expenses report but can build new reports using the Expenses semantic model.

**Answer Area:**

| Department  | Answer                                                               |
|-------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Management  | Assign the Viewer role for the workspace to each management department user |
| Accounting  | Publish an app                                                       |

**Correct Answer: Assign the Viewer role / Publish an app**

---

## Question #382

You have a PBIX file that contains sensitive information. You need to secure the information so that:

- Only internal users must be able to open the file if the file is shared via email.
- Once the file is published, only internal users must be able to open the semantic model and the report downloaded from the Power BI service.

**What should you do?**

- **A) From Microsoft Power BI Desktop, apply a sensitivity label. ✅ (Correct)**
- B) Certify the semantic model and the report.
- C) Use a live connection for the file.
- D) From the Power BI service, apply a sensitivity label.

**Correct Answer: A**

## 9. PL300-Practice-Questions.md

```md
# Microsoft PL-300 Practice Questions
> Source: SecExams.com — *Focus Only on What's Needed to Pass!*

---

## Question #1 — Storage Modes (HOTSPOT)

**Scenario:** You plan to create a Power BI model with four tables: Sales, Date, Customer, and SalesAggregate.

**Refresh requirements:**
- Customer: refreshed daily
- Date: refreshed once every three years
- Sales: refreshed in near real time
- SalesAggregate: refreshed once per week

**Requirements:** Minimize load times of visuals; ensure data is loaded based on refresh requirements.

**Correct Answers:**
| Table | Storage Mode |
|-------|-------------|
| Customer | Dual |
| Date | Dual |
| Sales | DirectQuery |
| SalesAggregate | Import |

**Explanation:**
- **Dual** — Tables can act as either cached or not cached depending on query context. Dimension tables (Customer, Date) are set to Dual to reduce limited relationships and improve performance.
- **DirectQuery** — Sales requires near real-time refresh; DirectQuery tables are not cached and queries are executed on-demand.
- **Import** — SalesAggregate is refreshed weekly; imported tables are cached and queries are fulfilled from cached data.

---

## Question #2 — Power BI Connector for Power Apps (Teams)

**Scenario:** You have a project management app fully hosted in Microsoft Teams, developed using Microsoft Power Apps. You need to create a Power BI report that connects to it.

**Which connector should you select?**

- A) Microsoft Teams Personal Analytics
- B) SQL Server database
- **C) Dataverse ✅ (Correct Answer)**
- D) Dataflows

**Explanation:** Microsoft Dataverse for Teams is a built-in, low-code data platform for Microsoft Teams. Power Apps data is stored in Dataverse; use the Dataverse connector in Power BI Desktop.

---

## Question #3 — Data Source for Existing Report (Minimize Effort)

**Scenario:** You publish a Power BI report that imports data from a Microsoft Excel file in a SharePoint folder. The data model contains several measures. You need to create a Power BI report from the existing data, minimizing development effort.

**Which type of data source should you use?**

- A) Power BI dataset
- **B) A SharePoint folder ✅ (Correct Answer)**
- C) Power BI dataflows
- D) An Excel workbook

**Explanation:** Connect to a SharePoint folder from Power Query Desktop to access the existing Excel file and reuse the existing data model and measures.

---

## Question #4 — Merging Tables in Power Query

**Scenario:** You import Customer (CustomerID, CustomerName, Phone, EmailAddress, AddressID) and Address (AddressID, AddressLine1, AddressLine2, City, State/Region, Country, PostalCode) tables. You need one row per customer with City, State/Region, and Country.

**What should you do?**

- **A) Merge the Customer and Address tables. ✅ (Correct Answer)**
- B) Group the Customer and Address tables by the Address ID column.
- C) Transpose the Customer and Address tables.
- D) Append the Customer and Address tables.

**Explanation:** A merge queries operation joins two existing tables based on matching values from one or multiple columns — equivalent to a SQL JOIN. (Append = UNION; Merge = JOIN.)

---

## Question #5 — Combining Tables from Two Databases (HOTSPOT)

**Scenario:** You have two Azure SQL databases with identical Customer table structures. You need to combine them into a single table, minimize data model size, and support scheduled refresh in powerbi.com.

**Correct Answers:**
| Action | Answer |
|--------|--------|
| Option to combine Customer tables | Append Queries as New |
| Action on original two SQL database queries | Disable loading the query to the data model |

**Explanation:**
- **Append Queries as New** creates a new query by appending multiple tables (UNION), keeping originals intact.
- **Disable loading the query to the data model** prevents the two original queries from loading into memory (reducing model size) while still allowing them to serve as intermediate sources for the combined query.

---

## Question #6 — Merge Join Types (DRAG DROP)

**Scenario:** In Power Query Editor, you have ProductCategory, ProductSubCategory, and Product queries. Every Product has a ProductSubCategory. Not every ProductSubCategory has a parent ProductCategory.

**Correct Answers:**
| Left Table | Right Table | Join Kind |
|------------|-------------|-----------|
| Product | ProductSubCategory | **Inner** |
| ProductSubCategory | ProductCategory | **Left outer** |

**Explanation:**
- **Inner join** — Every Product has a ProductSubCategory (all rows match); inner join gives best performance.
- **Left outer join** — Not every ProductSubCategory has a parent ProductCategory; left outer keeps all ProductSubCategory rows and brings in matching ProductCategory rows.

---

## Question #7 — Merging Queries for Performance

**Scenario:** You build a Power BI report from Azure SQL database erp1 with three tables: Products (product catalog), Orders (high-level order info), Order Line Items (productID, quantity, price). You need to analyze orders over time with total order value and orders by product attributes. Solution must minimize update times when interacting with visuals.

**What should you do first?**

- A) From Power Query, merge the Order Line Items query and the Products query.
- B) Create a calculated column that adds product categories to Orders using a DAX function.
- C) Calculate the count of orders per product using a DAX function.
- **D) From Power Query, merge the Orders query and the Order Line Items query. ✅ (Correct Answer)**

**Explanation:** Merging Orders and Order Line Items in Power Query (flattening the header/detail schema) is the most optimal approach for a star schema model.

---

## Question #8 — Loading Data from SharePoint

**Scenario:** A SharePoint Online site has document libraries. One library contains manufacturing reports saved as Excel files (same data structure). You need to load only the manufacturing reports.

**What should you do?**

- **A) Get data from a SharePoint folder, enter the site URL, select Transform, then filter by the folder path to the manufacturing reports library. ✅ (Correct Answer)**
- B) Get data from a SharePoint list, enter the site URL, select Combine & Transform, then filter by folder path.
- C) Get data from a SharePoint folder, enter the site URL, then select Combine & Load.
- D) Get data from a SharePoint list, enter the site URL, then select Combine & Load.

**Explanation:** Use SharePoint folder (not list) to access files in a document library. Use Transform (not Combine & Load) to filter by folder path before loading.

---

## Question #9 — Combining Tables with No Duplicates (DRAG DROP)

**Scenario:** An Excel workbook has Sheet1 with Table1 (Products: abc, def, ghi, jkl, mno) and Sheet2 with Table2 (Products: abc, xyz, tuv, mno, pqr, stu). You need to combine into a single column with no duplicates.

**Correct sequence (3 actions):**
1. From Power BI Desktop, import the data from Excel, and select Table1 and Table2.
2. From Power Query Editor, append Table2 to Table1.
3. From Power Query Editor, select Table1, and then select **Remove duplicates**.

---

## Question #10 — Date Column Transformation

**Scenario:** A CSV file has a column named `Logged` with values like `2018-12-31 at 08:59`. You need to analyze complaints by logged date and use a built-in date hierarchy.

**What should you do?**

- A) Apply a transformation to extract the last 11 characters and set data type to Date.
- B) Change the data type of the Logged column to Date.
- **C) Split the Logged column by using "at" as the delimiter. ✅ (Correct Answer)**
- D) Apply a transformation to extract the first 11 characters.

**Explanation:** Splitting by the "at" delimiter separates date and time into separate columns, enabling date hierarchy. CSV files have no inherent data types, so simple type conversion won't work on non-standard formats.

---

## Question #11 — Connectors for OneDrive Refresh

**Scenario:** You have a Microsoft Excel file in a OneDrive folder. It must be imported to a Power BI dataset that can be refreshed in powerbi.com.

**Which two connectors can you use?**

- **A) Excel Workbook ✅**
- B) Text/CSV
- **C) Folder ✅**
- D) SharePoint folder
- E) Web

**Explanation:** Both the Excel Workbook connector and Folder connector support connecting to OneDrive files and allow scheduled refresh in the Power BI service.

---

## Question #12 — Data Profiling (HOTSPOT)

**Scenario:** A table named Reports has a column named State. The data profiling shows: Valid 96%, Error 0%, Empty 4%. The column profile shows **69 distinct, 4 unique**.

**Correct Answers:**
| Statement | Answer |
|-----------|--------|
| There are [?] different values in State including nulls. | **69** |
| There are [?] non-null values that occur only once in State. | **4** |

**Explanation:**
- **Distinct** = total number of different values found in the column (including nulls) = 69
- **Unique** = total number of values that appear only once = 4

---

## Question #13 — Combining CSV Queries (HOTSPOT)

**Scenario:** You have Products.csv (ProductID, ProductName, SupplierID, CategoryID) and Categories.csv (CategoryID, CategoryName, CategoryDescription). You need a single table named Product with all six columns.

**Correct Answers:**
| Action | Answer |
|--------|--------|
| Combine the queries by performing a: | **Merge** |
| On the Categories query: | **Disable the query load** |

**Explanation:**
- **Merge** — Adding columns from one query to another = merge (JOIN). Append = adding rows (UNION).
- **Disable the query load** — Since the Categories data is now merged into Product, the standalone Categories query doesn't need to load into the model, saving memory.

---

## Question #14 — Near Real-Time Data Connectivity

**Scenario:** You have an Azure SQL database with frequently updated sales transactions. You need to detect fraudulent transactions and the data must be visible within five minutes of an update.

**How should you configure the data connection?**

- A) Add a SQL statement.
- B) Set the Command timeout in minutes setting.
- C) Set Data Connectivity mode to Import.
- **D) Set Data Connectivity mode to DirectQuery. ✅ (Correct Answer)**

**Explanation:** DirectQuery does not import or copy data into Power BI Desktop. It queries the underlying data source directly, so you're always viewing current data — ideal for near real-time requirements.

---

## Question #15 — File Metadata from Folder (DRAG DROP)

**Scenario:** You have a folder containing 100 CSV files. You need to make the file metadata available as a single dataset using Power BI. The solution must NOT store the data of the CSV files.

**Correct sequence (3 actions):**
1. From Power BI Desktop, select **Get Data**, and then select **Folder**.
2. From Power Query Editor, **expand the Attributes column**.
3. From Power Query Editor, **combine the Content column**.

---

## Question #16 — Dataflow Connection Mode

**Scenario:** A BI developer creates a dataflow in Power BI using DirectQuery to access on-premises SQL Server tables. The Enhanced Dataflows Compute Engine is on. Requirements: minimize online processing, minimize calculation/render times, include data from the current year up to the previous day.

**What should you do?**

- A) Create a dataflows connection with DirectQuery mode selected.
- B) Create a dataflows connection with DirectQuery mode and configure a gateway.
- **C) Create a dataflows connection with Import mode selected and schedule a daily refresh. ✅ (Correct Answer)**
- D) Create a dataflows connection with Import mode selected and create a Power Automate solution to refresh hourly.

**Explanation:** Import mode with daily scheduled refresh minimizes online processing and reduces calculation/render times. A daily refresh satisfies the requirement of data up to the previous day.

---

## Question #17 — Analyzing String Length Distribution

**Scenario:** You are creating a report in Power BI Desktop. You loaded a data extract with a free text field named col1. You need to analyze the frequency distribution of the string lengths in col1. The solution must not affect the size of the model.

**What should you do?**

- **A) In the report, add a DAX calculated column that calculates the length of col1. ✅ (Correct Answer)**
- B) In the report, add a DAX function that calculates the average length of col1.
- C) From Power Query Editor, add a column that calculates the length of col1.
- D) From Power Query Editor, change the distribution for the Column profile to group by length for col1.

**Explanation:** The LEN DAX function returns the number of characters in a text string. A DAX calculated column allows frequency distribution analysis. (Note: community discussion suggests Option D may also be valid as it doesn't store data.)

---

## Question #18 — Row-Level Security with App Distribution

**Scenario:** You have HR reports using row-level security (RLS). Multiple sales regions each have an HR manager. Managers must only see their region's data and must be prevented from changing report layout.

**How should you provision access?**

- **A) Publish the reports in an app and grant the HR managers access permission. ✅ (Correct Answer)**
- B) Create a new workspace, copy the datasets and reports, add HR managers as members.
- C) Publish the reports to a different workspace than the one hosting the datasets.
- D) Add the HR managers as members of the existing workspace.

**Explanation:** Publishing as an app prevents consumers from modifying the report layout. The RLS configuration ensures managers only see data for their region. **Key rule: when the requirement says "prevent changing layout," use an app.**

---

## Question #19 — Workspace Role Assignment (Least Privilege)

**Scenario:** You need to provide a user with the ability to add members to a workspace. The solution must use the principle of least privilege.

**Which role should you assign?**

- A) Viewer
- B) Admin
- C) Contributor
- **D) Member ✅ (Correct Answer)**

**Explanation:** The Member role allows adding members or other users with lower permissions to the workspace. Admin also has this ability but violates the principle of least privilege.

| Capability | Admin | Member | Contributor | Viewer |
|------------|-------|--------|-------------|--------|
| Update and delete the workspace | ✅ | | | |
| Add/remove people, including other admins | ✅ | | | |
| Allow Contributors to update the app | ✅ | | | |
| **Add members or others with lower permissions** | ✅ | **✅** | | |

---

## Question #20 — Reducing Query Load Times

**Scenario:** A Power BI query named Sales contains: ID, Sale_Date (datetime), Customer_ID, Delivery_Time (nullable), Status (Finished/Canceled), Canceled_Date (nullable). Only rows with Status = Finished are used. You need to reduce load times without affecting analysis.

**Which two actions achieve this goal?**

- **A) Remove the rows in which Sales[Status] has a value of Canceled. ✅**
- B) Remove Sales[Sales_Date].
- C) Change the data type of Sale[Delivery_Time] to Integer.
- **D) Split Sales[Sale_Date] into separate date and time columns. ✅**
- E) Remove Sales[Canceled_Date].

**Explanation:**
- **A** — Removing unneeded rows increases query performance.
- **D** — Splitting a DateTime column into Date and Time has a huge impact on data model size and is a best practice for performance optimization.

---

## Question #21 — Table Relationship Type

**Scenario:** You have Customer (CustomerID PK, Name, State, Email) and Transaction (TransactionID PK, CustomerID FK, Date, Amount) tables. You import both tables. Which relationship should you use to link them?

- A) one-to-many from Transaction to Customer
- B) one-to-one between Customer and Transaction
- C) many-to-many between Customer and Transaction
- **D) one-to-many from Customer to Transaction ✅ (Correct Answer)**

**Explanation:** One customer can have many transactions. The "one" side is on the primary key (CustomerID in Customer), and the "many" side is on the foreign key (CustomerID in Transaction).

---

## Question #22 — Minimizing Model Size and Preventing Email Access

**Scenario:** A custom connector returns ID, From, To, Subject, Body, and HasAttachments for 10M+ email records. You analyze employee internal networks (who emails whom). You need to prevent report recipients from reading the emails AND minimize model size.

**What should you do?**

- A) From Model view, set the Subject and Body columns to Hidden.
- **B) Remove the Subject and Body columns during the import. ✅ (Correct Answer)**
- C) Implement row-level security (RLS).

**Explanation:** Removing columns during import ensures they're not included in the model at all — this both prevents access (data is never stored) and minimizes model size. Hiding columns still stores the data in the model. RLS doesn't prevent access to the email content itself.

---

## Question #23 — Featured Table for Excel (HOTSPOT)

**Scenario:** You create a Power BI dataset with a Business Unit table (Cost Center, Headcount, ID, Name). You need to make the table available as an organizational data type in Microsoft Excel.

**Correct Answers:**
| Property | Answer |
|----------|--------|
| Row label | **Name** (or Cost Center — used to identify the row in Excel) |
| Key column | **ID** (provides the unique ID for the row) |
| Is featured table | **Yes** |

**Explanation:**
- **Row label** — Appears as the cell value in Excel's Data Selector pane and Information card. Should be a human-readable identifier (Name or Cost Center).
- **Key column** — Provides the unique ID enabling Excel to link a cell to a specific row.
- **Is featured table: Yes** — Makes the table discoverable in Excel's Data Types Gallery.

---

## Question #24 — Row-Level Security with Bidirectional Filtering

**Scenario:** The Power BI model has Country, Calendar, Purchase, and Purchase Detail tables. A manager represents only a single country. RLS requirements: managers see only their country's data; minimize number of RLS roles.

**Which two actions should you perform?**

- **A) Create a single role that filters Country[Manager_Email] by using the USERNAME DAX function. ✅**
- B) Create a single role that filters Country[Manager_Email] by using the USEROBJECTID DAX function.
- **C) For the relationship between Purchase Detail and Purchase, select Apply security filter in both directions. ✅**
- D) Create one role for each country.
- E) For the relationship between Purchase and Purchase Detail, change the Cross filter direction to Single.

**Explanation:**
- **A** — Dynamic RLS using `USERNAME()` creates a single role that filters based on the logged-in user's identity, minimizing role count.
- **C** — By default, RLS uses single-directional filters. To filter Purchase Detail through Purchase → Country, you must enable bidirectional security filtering on the Purchase Detail ↔ Purchase relationship.

---

## Question #25 — Data Model Schema and Performance (HOTSPOT)

**Scenario:** You have a Power BI imported dataset with Employee as the central fact table, surrounded by dimension tables: SeparationReason, Ethnicity, Date, PayType, BU, AgeGroup, Gender, FP.

**Correct Answers:**
| Statement | Answer |
|-----------|--------|
| Changing the [?] setting of the relationships will improve report query performance. | **Assume Referential Integrity** |
| The data model is organized into a [?]. | **Star schema** |

**Explanation:**
- **Assume Referential Integrity** — When using DirectQuery, this enables more efficient queries against the data source. *(Note: the question states imported data, so community discussion suggests Cross filter direction may also be applicable.)*
- **Star schema** — A mature modeling approach with a central fact table (Employee) surrounded by dimension tables.

---

## Question #26 — DAX Time Intelligence (HOTSPOT)

**Scenario:** You have a Sales table with a measure named Total Sales and a related date table. You need a measure that calculates total sales from the equivalent month of the previous year.

**Complete the DAX formula:**
```
Sales Previous Year =
CALCULATE (
    [Total Sales],
    PARALLELPERIOD (
        'Date'[Month],
        -1,
        year
    )
)
```

**Correct Answers:**
| Box | Answer |
|-----|--------|
| Box 1 | **CALCULATE** |
| Box 2 | **PARALLELPERIOD** |
| Box 3 | **'Date'[Month]** |

**Explanation:**
- **CALCULATE** — Evaluates a measure in a modified filter context.
- **PARALLELPERIOD** — Returns dates shifted a number of intervals (year/quarter/month) back or forward. Requires 3 arguments: dates column, number of intervals, interval type.
- **SAMEPERIODLASTYEAR** would also work but only takes 1 argument, and the answer structure requires 3 arguments.

---

## Question #27 — Implementing Row-Level Security (DRAG DROP)

**Scenario:** You plan to create a report displaying sales data for multiple regions. You need to restrict access to individual rows on a per region-basis using roles.

**Correct sequence (4 actions):**
1. **Import the data to Power BI Desktop.**
2. **Create a role definition.**
3. **Publish the report.**
4. **Assign users to the role.**

**Explanation:** Role definitions are created in Power BI Desktop. Users are assigned to roles in the Power BI Service (not Desktop). Publishing happens before assigning users.

---

## Question #28 — Denormalizing a Complex Model (DRAG DROP)

**Scenario:** Model contains: Sales_Region (region_id, name), Region_Manager (region_id, manager_id), Sales_Manager (sales_manager_id, name, region_id), Manager (manager_id, name). Relationships: one-to-one between Sales_Region and Region_Manager; more records in Manager than Region_Manager but every Region_Manager record exists in Manager; more records in Sales_Manager than Sales_Region but every Sales_Region record has a corresponding record in Sales_Manager. Only managers associated with a sales region must be included.

**Correct sequence (3 actions):**
1. **Merge [Sales_Region] and [Sales_Manager] by using an inner join.**
2. **Merge [Region_Manager] and [Manager] by using an inner join.**
3. **Merge [Sales_Region] and [Region_Manager] by using a right join as a new query named [Sales_Region_and_Region_Manager].**

**Explanation:**
- Inner join on Sales_Region × Sales_Manager ensures only regions with matching managers are included.
- Inner join on Region_Manager × Manager filters to only managers associated with a region.
- Right join combines the results into the final denormalized table.

---

*End of Questions 1–28 | Full document contains 382 questions across 874 pages.*
```

## 10. PL300-81-152.md

```md
# Microsoft PL-300 Practice Questions (Pages 81–152)

---

## Question #29

You have a Microsoft Power BI report. The size of PBIX file is 550 MB. The report is accessed by using an App workspace in shared capacity of powerbi.com. The report uses an imported dataset that contains one fact table. The fact table contains 12 million rows. The dataset is scheduled to refresh twice a day at 08:00 and 17:00. The report is a single page that contains 15 AppSource visuals and 10 default visuals. Users say that the report is slow to load the visuals when they access and interact with the report. You need to recommend a solution to improve the performance of the report. What should you recommend?

- A) Change any DAX measures to use iterator functions.
- B) Enable visual interactions.
- C) Replace the default visuals with AppSource visuals.
- **D) Split the visuals onto multiple pages. ✅ (Correct Answer)**

**Explanation:** One page with many visuals may also make your report loading slow. Please appropriately reduce the number of visualizations on one page.

---

## Question #30 (HOTSPOT)

You are creating a Microsoft Power BI imported data model to perform basket analysis. The goal of the analysis is to identify which products are usually bought together in the same transaction across and within sales territories. You import a fact table named **Sales** with the following columns:

| Column Name | Data Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| SalesRowID | Integer | Unique combination of SalesOrderNumber and SalesOrderLineNumber |
| ProductKey | Integer | Surrogate key to product dimension |
| OrderDateKey | Integer | Surrogate key to date dimension (YYYYMMDD) |
| OrderDate | Datetime | Date and time an order was processed |
| CustomerKey | Integer | Surrogate key to customer dimension |
| SalesTerritoryKey | Integer | Surrogate key to sales territory dimension |
| SalesOrderNumber | Text | Unique identifier of an order |
| SalesOrderLineNumber | Integer | Unique identifier of a line within an order |
| OrderQuantity | Integer | Quantity of the product ordered |
| LineTotal | Decimal | Total sales amount of a line before tax |
| TaxAmt | Decimal | Amount of tax charged |
| Freight | Decimal | Amount of freight charged |
| LastModified | Datetime | Date and time a row was last modified |
| AuditID | Integer | ID of the data load process |

You are evaluating how to optimize the model. For each statement, select Yes if true, otherwise No.

| Statement | Answer |
|---|---|
| The SalesRowID and AuditID columns can be removed without impeding the analysis goals. | **Yes** |
| Both the OrderDateKey and OrderDate columns are necessary to perform the basket analysis. | **No** |
| The TaxAmt column must retain the current number of decimal places to perform the basket analysis. | **No** |

**Explanation:**
- Box 1: Yes – Those two columns are not needed in the analysis.
- Box 2: No – Can remove the surrogate key OrderDateKey from the analysis.
- Box 3: No – Tax charged is not relevant for the analysis.

---

## Question #31

You have a Microsoft Power BI data model with three tables: **Orders**, **Date**, and **City**. There is a one-to-many relationship between Date and Orders, and between City and Orders. The model contains two RLS roles:
- **Role1** filter: `City[State Province] = "Kentucky"`
- **Role2** filter: `Date[Calendar Year] = 2020`

If a user is a member of both Role1 and Role2, what data will they see?

- A) Data where State Province is Kentucky OR Calendar Year is 2020.
- B) The user will receive an error.
- C) Only data where State Province is Kentucky.
- **D) Only data where State Province is Kentucky AND Calendar Year is 2020. ✅ (Correct Answer)**

**Explanation:** Row-level security (RLS) with Power BI can be used to restrict data access. Both roles are applied, and both role filters must be met.

> **Note:** Multiple role mappings make RLS filters ADDITIVE — users see the UNION of filters, not the intersection. Community discussion suggests the correct answer may actually be **A** based on Microsoft's RLS guidance.

---

## Question #39

In Power BI Desktop, you are building a sales report with two tables, both with RLS configured. You need to create a relationship ensuring bidirectional cross-filtering honors the RLS settings.

- A) Create an inactive relationship and select Apply security filter in both directions.
- **B) Create an active relationship and select Apply security filter in both directions. ✅ (Correct Answer)**
- C) Create an inactive relationship and select Assume referential integrity.
- D) Create an active relationship and select Assume referential integrity.

**Explanation:** By default, RLS filtering uses single-directional filters. Enable bidirectional cross-filtering with RLS by checking "Apply security filter in both directions." RLS works through ACTIVE relationships only.

| When UnitsInStock is added to a table visual as the only field, there will be ___ in the table. | **75 rows** |
| Changing the Summarize by setting and then adding the column to a table visual will ___ the number of rows. | **Reduce** |

**Explanation:**
- Box 1: 75 rows — Is nullable allows NULL values in the column, so all 75 non-null values show as rows.
- Box 2: Reduce — Summarization will aggregate values, reducing rows.

## Question #41 (HOTSPOT)

You have a Power BI report with:
- **Balances** table: daily closing balances for every active bank account
- **Date** table: records per day for calendar years 2000–2025, with hierarchy for financial year, quarter, month, and day

DAX measure:
```
Accounts := CALCULATE(
    DISTINCTCOUNT(Balances[AccountID]),
    LASTDATE('Date'[Date])
)
```

| Statement | Answer |
|---|---|
| At year level, the [Accounts] measure shows accounts live throughout the year. | **No** |
| At month level, the [Accounts] measure shows accounts live throughout the month. | **No** |
| At day level, the [Accounts] measure shows accounts live that day. | **Yes** |

**Explanation:** LASTDATE returns the last date in the current context. At year level, it returns accounts live on the last day of the year only (not throughout the year). Same logic applies at month level.

- C) Create a calculated table containing Ad_id, Site_name, and Impression_date.
- D) Create a calculated measure that aggregates by using the COUNTROWS function.

**Explanation:** Grouping in Power Query reduces rows loaded into the model. Creating relationships doesn't increase model size. A calculated table would increase model size.

## 11. PL300-153-219.md

```md
# Microsoft PL-300 Practice Questions (Questions #59–#88)

---

## Question #59 (HOTSPOT)

You have a Power BI report that contains a measure named Total Sales. You need to create a new measure that will return the sum of Total Sales for a year up to a selected date.

How should you complete the DAX expression?

**Correct Answer:**

```
Measure = TOTALYTD([Total Sales], 'Date'[Date])
```

- **Box 1:** `TOTALYTD` — evaluates the specified expression over the interval which begins on the first day of the year and ends with the last date in the specified date column after applying specified filters.
- **Box 2:** `'Date'[Date]` — provides the date context for the calculation.

---

## Question #60 (DRAG DROP)

You are modifying a Power BI model using Power BI Desktop. You have a table named **Sales** with fields: Transaction ID, Customer Key, Sales Date Key, Sales Amount. You have a table named **Transaction Size** with: Transaction Size ID, Transaction Size, Min, Max.

You need to create a calculated column to classify each transaction as small, medium, or large based on Sales Amount.

**Correct Answer:**

```dax
Transaction Size =
VAR SalesTotal = 'Sales'[Sales]
VAR FilterSegment =
    CALCULATE(
        'Transaction Size',
        AND(
            'Transaction Size'[Min] <= SalesTotal,
            'Transaction Size'[Max] >= SalesTotal
        )
    )
VAR Result =
    FILTER(DISTINCT('Transaction Size'[Transaction Size]), FilterSegment)
RETURN
    Result
```

- **Box 1:** `CALCULATE`
- **Box 2:** `AND`
- **Box 3:** `FILTER`

---

## Question #61

- **B) Remove the LineItems[Description] column. ✅ (Correct Answer)**
- C) Remove the rows from LineItems where Invoice Date is before last month.
- D) Group LineItems by Invoice ID and Invoice Date with a sum of Price.

**Explanation:** Removing a column not used in the visuals reduces dataset size. Merging increases it; two visuals need historical data; grouping doesn't affect size.

- **D) Remove Posts[Full Text] and Posts[Summary]. ✅ (Correct Answer)**
- E) Remove rows in Traffic where Referring URL does not start with "/".

## Question #81 (DRAG DROP)

You need a measure for percent of total sales per product, respecting the report-level filter.

**Correct Answer:**

```dax
Percent of Product Sales =
VAR ProductSales = SUM('Sales'[Sales])
VAR AllSales =
    CALCULATE(SUM('Sales'[Sales]), FILTER('Products'[Product]))
RETURN
    DIVIDE(ProductSales, AllSales)
```

- **Box 1:** `CALCULATE`
- **Box 2:** `FILTER`

You need a calculated column **Active Store Name**: return Store Name if Status = "A", else return "Inactive - " + Store Name.

**Correct Answer:**

```dax
Active Store Name = IF([Status] = "A", [Store Name], "Inactive - " & [Store Name])
```

- **Box 1:** `IF`
- **Box 2:** `&`

## 12. PL300-220-279.md

```md
# Microsoft PL-300 Practice Questions (Pages 220–279)

---

## Question #89

You have a CSV file that contains user complaints. The file contains a column named **Logged**. Logged contains the date and time each complaint occurred. The data in Logged is in the following format: `2018-12-31 at 08:59`.

You need to be able to analyze the complaints by the logged date and use a built-in date hierarchy.

**What should you do?**

- A) Change the data type of the Logged column to Date.
- **B) Split the Logged column by using `at` as the delimiter. ✅ (Correct Answer)**
- C) Add a conditional column that outputs 2018 if the Logged column starts with 2018 and set the data type of the new column to Whole Number.
- D) Apply the Parse function from the Date transformations options to the Logged column.

### Explanation

Splitting using the custom delimiter `" at "` creates two fields and Power BI automatically recognizes the date as a date type, hence the date hierarchy will be available.

---

## Question #90 — HOTSPOT

You have the Power BI data model shown in the exhibit (Product → Sales ← Date).

You need to create a measure to count the number of product categories that had products sold during a selected period.

**How should you complete the DAX expression?**

```
Product Categories Sold =
CALCULATE (
    DISTINCTCOUNT('Product'[ProductCategory]),   <-- First dropdown
    'Sales'                                       <-- Second dropdown
)
```

| Slot | Correct Selection |
|------|------------------|
| Function | `DISTINCTCOUNT('Product'[ProductCategory])` |
| Filter table | `'Sales'` |

### Explanation

`DISTINCTCOUNT` is used to count unique product categories. `COUNT` would double-count. The filter context comes from the `'Sales'` table (not `'Date'`), which links sales activity to the selected period.

---

## Question #91 — HOTSPOT

You have the Azure SQL databases shown in the following table:

| Name | Stage | Server URL |
|------|-------|------------|
| db-powerbi-dev | Development | dev.database.windows.net |
| db-powerbi-uat | Test | uat.database.windows.net |
| db-powerbi-prod | Production | prod.database.windows.net |

You plan to build a single PBIX file to meet the following requirements:
- Data must be consumed from the database that corresponds to each stage of the development lifecycle.
- Power BI deployment pipelines must **NOT** be used.
- The solution must minimize administrative effort.

**What should you do?**

| Setting | Answer |
|---------|--------|
| Create: | **One parameter** ✅ |
| Parameter type: | **Text** ✅ |

### Explanation

Create a single Text parameter with a suggested list of values: `dev`, `uat`, `prod`. Use it in the Power Query connection string:

```
Source = Sql.Database(#"ParameterName"&".database.windows.net", "db-powerbi-"&#"ParameterName")
```

This allows switching between environments with minimal effort.

---

## Question #92

You are creating a query to be used as a Country dimension in a star schema.

Source data (snapshot):

| Country | City |
|---------|------|
| USA | Seattle |
| USA | New York |
| USA | Denver |
| UK | Manchester |
| UK | London |
| Japan | Tokyo |
| Brazil | Rio |
| Brazil | Sao Paulo |

The dimension must contain a list of **unique countries**.

**Which two actions should you perform?**

- A) Delete the Country column.
- B) Remove duplicates from the table.
- C) Remove duplicates from the City column.
- **D) Delete the City column. ✅ (Correct Answer)**
- **E) Remove duplicates from the Country column. ✅ (Correct Answer)**

### Explanation

Delete the City column (not needed), then remove duplicates from the Country column to get a unique list.

---

## Question #93

You have a Microsoft Power BI report. The PBIX file is 550 MB. The report is accessed via an App workspace in shared capacity. It uses an imported dataset with one fact table of 12 million rows, refreshed twice daily. The report page contains 15 AppSource visuals and 10 default visuals. Users report slow load times.

**What should you recommend?**

- A) Enable visual interactions.
- B) Change any DAX measures to use iterator functions.
- C) Implement row-level security (RLS).
- **D) Remove unused columns from tables in the data model. ✅ (Correct Answer)**

---

## Question #94 — HOTSPOT

You have the Power BI data model (Product ↔ Sales ↔ Date). The Sales table columns are:

| Name | Data type | Sample value |
|------|-----------|--------------|
| ProductID | Whole number | 1 |
| InvoiceNumber | Whole number | 100005 |
| OrderDate | Date | 2022-05-09 |
| ShipDate | Date | 2022-05-12 |
| SalesAmount | Decimal number | 1500.75 |
| SalesQuantity | Whole number | 3 |
| LastUpdated | Date/time | 5/22/2022 11:45:30 AM |
| AuditID | Whole number | 123212 |

Required analysis:
- Total sales by product by **month** in which the order was placed
- Quantities sold by product by **day** on which the order was placed
- Number of sales transactions by **quarter** in which the order was placed

**For each statement, select Yes or No:**

| Statement | Answer |
|-----------|--------|
| Removing the LastUpdated column reduces model size while still supporting the required analysis. | **Yes** ✅ |
| Removing the ProductID column reduces model size while still supporting the required analysis. | **No** ✅ |
| Removing the ShipDate column reduces model size while still supporting the required analysis. | **Yes** ✅ |

## Question #95

Same scenario as Q89 (Logged column format: `2018-12-31 at 08:59`).

**What should you do?**

- **A) Create a column by example that starts with 2018-12-31 and set the data type of the new column to Date. ✅ (Correct Answer)**
- B) Create a column by example that starts with 2018-12-31.
- C) Apply a transformation to extract the last 11 characters of the Logged column.
- D) Add a conditional column that outputs 2018 if the Logged column starts with 2018 and set the data type to Whole Number.

### Explanation

Using "Column from Example" to extract the date part requires manually setting the data type to Date afterward, since Power BI will not auto-detect it in this case.

---

## Question #96

You have a Power BI data model with an Employees table containing: Employee Name, Email Address, Start Date, Job Title.

You are implementing dynamic row-level security (RLS). Users must see only their own employee data. The DAX expression must work in both Power BI Desktop and the Power BI service.

**Which expression should you use?**

- A) [Email Address] = USERNAME()
- B) [Employee Name] = USERPRINCIPALNAME()
- C) [Employee Name] = USERNAME()
- **D) [Email Address] = USERPRINCIPALNAME() ✅ (Correct Answer)**

### Explanation

`USERPRINCIPALNAME()` always returns the user in email/UPN format (e.g., `user@contoso.com`) in both Desktop and the Service. `USERNAME()` returns `DOMAIN\username` format in Desktop, making it inconsistent.

---

## Question #97 — DRAG DROP

You have the Power BI data model with Country, Sales, and Human Resources tables.

Country table data:

| Country | Manager | Email |
|---------|---------|-------|
| USA | CFO | cfo@msn.com |
| France | Phillipe | phillipe@msn.com |
| Brazil | Juan | juan@msn.com |
| Singapore | Srini | srini@msn.com |

Two RLS roles: **Manager** and **CFO**.

Requirements:
- Each manager sees only their own country's data (Sales + Human Resources).
- The CFO must be **prevented** from seeing Human Resources data.
- The CFO must see **all countries'** sales data.

**DAX Filter Expressions:**

| Table Filter | Expression |
|---|---|
| Human Resources (CFO role) | `[Manager] = "CFO"` — **Note:** Use `False()` to block access |
| Country (Manager role) | `[Email] = userprincipalname()` |

### Correct Answer

| Target | DAX Expression |
|--------|---------------|
| Human Resources | `[Manager] = "CFO"` → effectively `False()` blocks CFO from HR |
| Country | `[Email] = userprincipalname()` |

---

## Question #98

You have a Power BI data model importing from a Microsoft Excel spreadsheet via Power Query (with renamed and custom columns). On reload you receive:

> `Expression.Error: The column 'Category' of the table wasn't found.`

**What are two possible causes?**

- **A) The column was removed from the source file. ✅ (Correct Answer)**
- **B) The column was renamed in the source file. ✅ (Correct Answer)**
- C) The file is no longer in the specified location.
- D) The data type of the column was changed.

## Question #99

You have a Power BI model with a Sales table containing: Order Line ID, Product ID, Unit Price, Order ID, Quantity.

Orders are uniquely identified by Order ID and can have multiple order lines.

**Which formula counts the number of orders?**

- A) Count('Sales'[Order ID])
- B) CountA('Sales'[Order ID])
- C) CountRows('Sales')
- **D) DistinctCount('Sales'[Order ID]) ✅ (Correct Answer)**

### Explanation

Since one Order ID can appear in multiple rows (multiple order lines), `DISTINCTCOUNT` is needed to count unique orders only.

---

## Question #100 — DRAG DROP

You use Power Query Editor to preview data with columns: SKU (text), price (number), discount (any type — 63% errors).

You need to clean the query so all rows are maintained and error values in discount are replaced with `0.05`.

**Which three actions in sequence?**

| Order | Action |
|-------|--------|
| 1 | Select the **discount** column. |
| 2 | Select **Replace Errors** to replace each error value with 0.05. |
| 3 | For the discount column, change Data Type to **Decimal Number**. |

### Explanation

Errors must be replaced before changing the data type, otherwise changing the type first may create or complicate errors.

---

## Question #101 — HOTSPOT

You attempt to create a custom column in Power Query Editor and receive:

> `Expression.Error: We cannot apply operator & to types Text and Number.`
> `Operator=& | Left=A | Right=1`

**Complete the statements:**

| Statement | Answer |
|-----------|--------|
| The error is caused by: | **Mismatched data types** |
| The desired outcome of the custom column is: | **A1** |

### Explanation

The `&` operator in Power Query concatenates text. Since the right side is a number (1), the types are mismatched. The intent was to produce `"A1"` (text concatenation of `"A"` and `"1"`).

---

Numbers = GENERATESERIES ( -100, 100, 1 )

## Question #104

You have Excel data with SKU, price, and discount columns. Some discount values are Excel errors (`#DIV/0!`, `#NAME?`, `#N/A`, `#NULL!`, `#NUM!`, `#REF!`, `#VALUE!`).

Requirements:
- If discount returns an error → use 0.05.
- All rows must be maintained.
- Minimize administrative effort.

## Question #105

In Power Query Editor, you have a query named **Sales Data** with columns: Sale date (Date), Product ID (Whole number), Product name (Text), Product category (Text), Customer ID (Whole number).

You need to create two queries named **Product Dimension** and **Sales Fact** based on Sales Data. The solution must minimize maintenance effort and dataset size.

**Which two actions should you perform?**

- **A) Reference the Sales Data query to create the new queries. ✅ (Correct Answer)**
- B) Disable the load for the Sales Fact query.
- C) Duplicate the Sales Data query to create the new queries.
- D) Clear Include in report refresh for the Sales Data query.
- **E) Disable the load for the Sales Data query. ✅ (Correct Answer)**

### Explanation

Referencing (not duplicating) avoids data duplication. Disabling load on the original Sales Data query prevents it from being loaded redundantly into the model.

---

## Question #106

You have a Power BI model with a Date table containing: Date, Fiscal Year, Fiscal Quarter, Month Name, Calendar Year, Week Number, Month Number, Calendar Quarter.

You need to create a calculated table with only **unique combinations** of Calendar Year, Calendar Quarter, and Calendar Month.

**Which DAX function should you use?**

- A) ADDCOLUMNS
- B) CALCULATE
- **C) SUMMARIZE ✅ (Correct Answer)**
- D) DATATABLE

### Example

```dax
UniqueCalendarTable = SUMMARIZE(
    Date,
    'Date'[Calendar Year],
    'Date'[Calendar Quarter],
    'Date'[Month Name]
)
```

---

You have a Power BI model with:

## Question #109 — HOTSPOT

You are creating a Power BI model to analyze inventory. Three tables: Date, Product, Inventory (one-to-many from Date and Product to Inventory). Inventory is recorded daily; the correct quantity for a month is the **last recorded value**.

```
Last Inventory Count =
CALCULATE (
    SUM ( 'Inventory'[QuantityAvailable] ),
    LASTDATE ( 'Date'[Date] )
)
```

|------|---------------|
| Outer function | **Calculate** |
| Date filter function | **LastDate** |

## Question #113

You use Power Query to import two tables: **Order Header** and **Order Details** from Azure SQL, related by **Order ID**. You need to combine them into a single query with the unique columns of each.

**What should you select in Power Query Editor?**

- **A) Merge queries ✅ (Correct Answer)**
- B) Combine files
- C) Append queries

### Explanation

Merge joins tables on a common key (Order ID), producing a single table with unique columns from each. Append stacks rows, not columns.

## Question #115 — HOTSPOT

You are creating a Power BI report showing the number of current employees over time (Import mode). Data is from Azure SQL in monthly snapshots stored in a **Headcount** table (one row per employee per month; a DateKey for the first day of each snapshot month). You have a related Date table for 2020–2030.

Headcount =
Calculate (
    (CountRows('Headcount')),
    LastDate('Date'[Date])
)

## Question #116 — HOTSPOT

## Question #117

You have a Power BI model with Date and Sales tables (same as Q107). An existing measure: `Total Sales = SUM(Sales[Sale])`.

You need to create a **Previous Quarter** measure showing sales one quarter before the selected period.

## Question #118

Same scenario as Q93 (550 MB PBIX, 12M rows, 15 AppSource + 10 default visuals, slow loads).

**What should you recommend?**

- A) Change any DAX measures to use iterator functions.
- B) Implement row-level security (RLS).
- C) Replace the default visuals with AppSource visuals.
- **D) Split the visuals onto multiple pages. ✅ (Correct Answer)**

### Explanation

All visuals on a page load simultaneously. Splitting across multiple pages reduces the number of queries fired at once, improving load time.
